I mean, I’m managing, mostly, but it’s been frustrating.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and has this been a constant feeling or have there been times when you felt better in between?\n\nPatient: Um… I’d say it’s been maybe about three months? It’s not like every single minute is awful or anything, and I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but this quieter, heavy feeling has been pretty steady. Like low interest in things and the fatigue. I might have a better day here and there, but overall it’s been hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest or pleasure in activities that you usually enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I have noticed that. It’s not like I never do anything, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking… even yoga or playing tennis with a friend. I keep telling myself I’m just in a rut, which maybe I am, but it feels like the enjoyment part just isn’t really there right now. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you’ve felt a loss of interest or pleasure in activities, like sketching or spending time with friends? Has it been nearly every day, most days, or only occasionally?\n\nPatient: I think… most days, probably. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still show up for things sometimes, but the caring part is really muted. Like with sketching, gallery openings, cooking, yoga, even tennis with a friend — those usually feel like “me,” and lately I’ve just kind of stopped being interested in them. Most days it’s there in some way, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling down or depressed most days, or having little hope about things improving?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that neatly. I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless, exactly… that sounds stronger than what I mean. But I have felt pretty down a lot of days, yes. I still kind of tell myself it’ll pass, so there’s some hope there, but it’s been harder to really feel that lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently? Like eating more or less than usual, or gaining or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um… a little, yeah. I don’t think it’s anything dramatic, but my appetite’s been off. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically just had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and I didn’t really notice until later.\n\nAnd my jeans are fitting looser, so I guess I’ve lost some weight without trying. I’m not trying to diet or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have been moving more slowly than usual, or that your movements have felt physically slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least on some days. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but there are days where my body feels like it’s moving through wet cement. Like everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nAnd even answering a simple question from my spouse can take me this weirdly long time, like I’m trying to get the words to catch up. Is that what you mean by slowed down?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a loss of energy or increased fatigue most days, even after resting or sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s actually a big part of why I’m here. I’m tired in this way that sleep doesn’t really fix. I’ll rest and still feel like… just doing something small, like unloading the dishwasher, feels like more than it should.\n\nEven trying to read for my certificate class can feel like too much sometimes, which makes me feel kind of bad saying it out loud. But yes, most days I feel pretty drained. I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless, exactly… that sounds stronger than what I mean. But I have felt pretty down a lot of days, yes. I still kind of tell myself it’ll pass, so there’s some hope there, but it’s been harder to really feel that lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently? I mean, I’m managing, mostly, but it’s been frustrating.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and has this been a constant feeling or have there been times when you felt better in between?\n\nPatient: Um… I’d say it’s been maybe about three months? It’s not like every single minute is awful or anything, and I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but this quieter, heavy feeling has been pretty steady. Like low interest in things and the fatigue. I might have a better day here and there, but overall it’s been hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest or pleasure in activities that you usually enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I have noticed that. It’s not like I never do anything, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking… even yoga or playing tennis with a friend. I keep telling myself I’m just in a rut, which maybe I am, but it feels like the enjoyment part just isn’t really there right now. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you’ve felt a loss of interest or pleasure in activities, like sketching or spending time with friends? Has it been nearly every day, most days, or only occasionally?\n\nPatient: I think… most days, probably. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still show up for things sometimes, but the caring part is really muted. Like with sketching, gallery openings, cooking, yoga, even tennis with a friend — those usually feel like “me,” and lately I’ve just kind of stopped being interested in them. Most days it’s there in some way, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling down or depressed most days, or having little hope about things improving?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that neatly. I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless, exactly… that sounds stronger than what I mean. But I have felt pretty down a lot of days, yes. I still kind of tell myself it’ll pass, so there’s some hope there, but it’s been harder to really feel that lately, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently? Like eating more or less than usual, or gaining or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um… a little, yeah. I don’t think it’s anything dramatic, but my appetite’s been off. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically just had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, and I didn’t really notice until later.\n\nAnd my jeans are fitting looser, so I guess I’ve lost some weight without trying. I’m not trying to diet or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have been moving more slowly than usual, or that your movements have felt physically slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least on some days. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but there are days where my body feels like it’s moving through wet cement. Like everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nAnd even answering a simple question from my spouse can take me this weirdly long time, like I’m trying to get the words to catch up. Is that what you mean by slowed down?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a loss of energy or increased fatigue most days, even after resting or sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that’s actually a big part of why I’m here. I’m tired in this way that sleep doesn’t really fix. I’ll rest and still feel like… just doing something small, like unloading the dishwasher, feels like more than it should.\n\nEven trying to read for my certificate class can feel like too much sometimes, which makes me feel kind of bad saying it out loud. But yes, most days I feel pretty drained. Is that the kind of fatigue you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your low mood or lack of energy has been present for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, because I’ve still had some okay moments, but this quieter low stretch has been around for a while.\n\nProbably about three months now, where most days I just feel kind of down and not interested in much, and the tiredness is there a lot of the day.
